ring_members : [&#x27;riak-node1@64.137.190.244&#x27;,&#x27;riak-node2@64.137.247.82&#x27;,
&#x27;riak-node3@64.137.162.64&#x27;,&#x27;riak-node4@64.137.161.229&#x27;,
&#x27;riak-node5@64.137.217.73&#x27;]

Are these nodes in the same local area network?

On Thu, Feb 9, 2017 at 12:49 PM, my hue &lt;tranmyhue.grac...@gmail.com&gt; wrote:
<blockquote>Dear Russel,

I did the simplest possible with new document and use modify_type to update
a single register.
I still meet fail update at some times.

My steps did as follow :

Step 1:   Initial a new document Map
Step 2:  Create new map with :  riakc_pb_socket:update_type(Pid,
{BucketType, Bucket}, Key,  riakc_map:to_op(Map), []).
Step 3:   Fetch to check result :
riakc_pb_socket:fetch_type(Pid,{BucketType,Bucket}, Key).
Step 4:  Create Fun for input of modify_type which update only one field of
map

Fun1 = fun(OldMap) -&gt; riakc_map:update({&lt;&lt;&quot;status_id&quot;&gt;&gt;, register}, fun(R)
-&gt; riakc_register:set(&lt;&lt;&quot;show&quot;&gt;&gt;,  R) end, OldMap) end.

Fun2 = fun(OldMap) -&gt; riakc_map:update({&lt;&lt;&quot;status_id&quot;&gt;&gt;, register}, fun(R)
-&gt; riakc_register:set(&lt;&lt;&quot;hide&quot;&gt;&gt;,  R) end, OldMap) end.

Step 5: Update :

riakc_pb_socket:modify_type(Pid, Fun1, {BucketType, Bucket}, Key, []).

Fetch to check :

riakc_pb_socket:fetch_type(Pid,{BucketType,Bucket}, Key).

Step 6:  Update:

riakc_pb_socket:modify_type(Pid, Fun2, {BucketType, Bucket}, Key, []).

Fetch to check :

riakc_pb_socket:fetch_type(Pid,{BucketType,Bucket}, Key).

For my debug and test,  I repeated step 5 and step 6 on one document about
20 times.
And via many documents, I meet weird behaviour that some documents meet fail
update, and some documents never fail update.
The first time, I think that cause network, or timeout between nodes and
this is only random of fail.  So I deleted documents with command:

riakc_pb_socket:delete(Pid, {BucketType,Bucket}, Key, []).

Then retest on each document of first test again. And It is very amazing
that the documents meet fail at first test still meet fail at this second
test, and the documents passed at first test still pass at this second test.
Delete all again, and retest and of course get the same result.

After all I make other test case, I used one fail document at all test
times, and keep all fields except change key to get different documents for
the debug.  And very surprise that I still got some fail and some success,
although documents are  the same field and value except key.  Delete and
retest and still the same result. Documents succeeded will be always
succeed. And document meet fail will be always failed.  I totally do not
understand root cause till now. And hope that can get support and help from
the developers of riak.   I can tell that my system mostly fail with cluster
run when faced this issue.

The following is some map documents I used on the test.  And I also attached
the extracted log of each node at one of the fail times together with this
email. I do not really get meaning of riak log but hope that can help
developers of riak get something.

bucket type and bucket.   Bucket Type and Bucket have properties with which
I reported on first email. So for remind, under is a description  of bucket
type, bucket and cluster :

* Bucket Type :

- Bucket type created with the following command:

riak-admin bucket-type create bucket_type_name
&#x27;{&quot;props&quot;:{&quot;backend&quot;:&quot;bitcask_mult&quot;,&quot;datatype&quot;:&quot;map&quot;}}&#x27;

riak-admin bucket-type activate bucket_type_name

<blockquote>Dear John and Russell Brown,

* How fast is your turnaround time between an update and a fetch?

The turnaround time between an update and a fetch about 1 second.
During my team and I  debug, we adjusted haproxy with the scenario
as follow:

Scenario 1 : round robin via 5 nodes of cluster

We meet issue at scenario 1 and we are afraid of that timeout can
be occurs between nodes,
make us still get stale data. Then we performed scenario 2

Scenario 2:  Disable round robin and only route request to node 1.
Cluster still is 5 nodes.
With this case we ensure that request update and fetch always come
to and from node 1.
And the issue still occurs.

At the fail time, I hoped that can get any error log from riak
nodes to give me any information.
But riak log show to me nothing and everything is ok.

riakc_map:to_op(Map), []).
riakc_pb_socket:fetch_type(Pid, {BucketType, Bucket}, Key, []).

On Tue, Feb 7, 2017 at 2:11 AM, John Daily &lt;jda...@basho.com&gt;
wrote:
Originally I suspected the context which allows Riak to resolve
conflicts was not present in your data, but I see it in your map
structure.
Thanks for supplying such a detailed description.

How fast is your turnaround time between an update and a fetch?
Even if the cluster is healthy it’s not impossible to see a timeout
between
nodes, which could result in a stale retrieval. Have you verified
whether
the stale data persists?

A single node cluster gives an advantage that you’ll never see in
a real cluster: a perfectly synchronized clock. It also reduces (but
does
not completely eliminate) the possibility of an internal timeout
between
processes.

-John

<blockquote>On Feb 6, 2017, at 1:02 PM, my hue &lt;tranmyhue.grac...@gmail.com&gt;
wrote:

Dear Riak Team,

I and my team used riak as database for my production with an
cluster including 5 nodes.
While production run, we meet an critical bug that is sometimes
fail to update document.
I and my colleagues performed debug and detected an issue with
the scenario as follow:

+  fetch document
+  change value of document
+  update document

Repeat about 10 times and will meet fail. With the document is
updated continually,
sometimes will face update fail.

The first time,  5 nodes of cluster we used riak version 2.1.1.
After meet above bug, we upgraded to use riak version 2.2.0 and
this issue still occurs.

Via many time test,  debug using  Tcpdump at riak node :

tcpdump -A -ttt  -i {interface} src host {host} and dst port
{port}

And together with the command:

riak-admin status | grep &quot;node_puts_map\| node_puts_map_total\|
node_puts_total\| vnode_map_update_total\| vnode_puts_total\&quot;

we  got that the riak server already get the update request.
However, do not know why riak backend fail to update document.
At the fail time,  from riak server log everything is ok.

Then we removed cluster and use a single riak server,  and see
that above bug never happen.

For that reason, think that is only happen with cluster work. We
took research on basho riak document and our riak configure
seems that like suggestion from document.  We totally blocked on
this issue and hope that can get support from you
so that can obtain a stable work from riak database for our
production.
Thank you so much.  Hope that can get your reply soon.

</div><ol class="mailing-list-replies"><li class="mailing-list-reply"><details id="msg17951" class="mailing-list-message"><summary><header class="mailing-list-message__header"><span class="mailing-list-message__author">DeadZen</span><time datetime="2017-02-09T12:23:32-08:00">February 9, 2017 at 12:23 PM</time><span class="mailing-list-message__subject">Re: [Basho Riak] Fail To Update Document Repeatly With Cluster of 5	Nodes</span></header></summary><div class="mailing-list-message__content"><div class="mailing-list-message__body">Why are they public?
</div><ol class="mailing-list-replies"><li class="mailing-list-reply"><details id="msg17952" class="mailing-list-message"><summary><header class="mailing-list-message__header"><span class="mailing-list-message__author">John Daily</span><time datetime="2017-02-10T00:20:12-08:00">February 10, 2017 at 12:20 AM</time><span class="mailing-list-message__subject">Re: [Basho Riak] Fail To Update Document Repeatly With Cluster of 5	Nodes</span></header></summary><div class="mailing-list-message__content"><div class="mailing-list-message__body">The questions about your IP addresses are good ones: you’re likely to run into
more trouble when a Riak cluster is spread across multiple networks, and from a
security standpoint I would recommend against exposing Riak KV to an untrusted
network, even if its security features are enabled.

Would you please try setting pw=3 and pr=3 on your writes and reads? I would
not be surprised to find that the objects which consistently succeed will
report success, while the objects that consistently have trouble will report an
error.

Decreasing those values to 2 would also be interesting: if a CRDT update
succeeds with pw=2 and a fetch succeeds with pr=2 and the data is still stale,
that would be of particular concern.

And it should not matter for this use case, but please check to see whether all
your server clocks are synchronized.

-John

<blockquote>On Feb 9, 2017, at 3:21 PM, DeadZen &lt;dead...@deadzen.com&gt; wrote:

Why are they public?
</blockquote></div></div></details></li><li class="mailing-list-reply"><details id="msg17953" class="mailing-list-message"><summary><header class="mailing-list-message__header"><span class="mailing-list-message__author">Nguyễn Văn Nghĩa Em</span><time datetime="2017-02-10T00:41:15-08:00">February 10, 2017 at 12:41 AM</time><span class="mailing-list-message__subject">Re: [Basho Riak] Fail To Update Document Repeatly With Cluster of 5	Nodes</span></header></summary><div class="mailing-list-message__content"><div class="mailing-list-message__body">Hi All,

These nodes are not in Local Area Network because our host provider doesn&#x27;t
have a local IP. It is only our DEV environments. If there are problems
with cluster I will setup a local environment to try.

On Tue, Feb 7, 2017 at 2:11 AM, John Daily &lt;jda...@basho.com&gt;
wrote:
Originally I suspected the context which allows Riak to resolve
conflicts was not present in your data, but I see it in your
</blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote>map structure.
<blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote>Thanks for supplying such a detailed description.

How fast is your turnaround time between an update and a fetch?
Even if the cluster is healthy it’s not impossible to see a
</blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote>timeout between
<blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote>nodes, which could result in a stale retrieval. Have you
</blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote>verified whether
<blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote>the stale data persists?

A single node cluster gives an advantage that you’ll never see
</blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote>in
<blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote>a real cluster: a perfectly synchronized clock. It also
</blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote>reduces (but does
<blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote>not completely eliminate) the possibility of an internal
</blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote>timeout between
<blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote>processes.

-John

<blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote>wrote:

Dear Riak Team,

I and my team used riak as database for my production with an
cluster including 5 nodes.
While production run, we meet an critical bug that is
</blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote>sometimes
<blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote>fail to update document.
I and my colleagues performed debug and detected an issue with
the scenario as follow:

+  fetch document
+  change value of document
+  update document

Repeat about 10 times and will meet fail. With the document is
updated continually,
sometimes will face update fail.

The first time,  5 nodes of cluster we used riak version
</blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote>2.1.1.
<blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote>After meet above bug, we upgraded to use riak version 2.2.0
</blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote>and
<blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote>this issue still occurs.

Via many time test,  debug using  Tcpdump at riak node :

tcpdump -A -ttt  -i {interface} src host {host} and dst port
{port}

And together with the command:

riak-admin status | grep &quot;node_puts_map\|
</blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote>node_puts_map_total\|
<blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote>node_puts_total\| vnode_map_update_total\| vnode_puts_total\&quot;

we  got that the riak server already get the update request.
However, do not know why riak backend fail to update document.
At the fail time,  from riak server log everything is ok.

Then we removed cluster and use a single riak server,  and see
that above bug never happen.

For that reason, think that is only happen with cluster work.
</blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote>We
<blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote>took research on basho riak document and our riak configure
seems that like suggestion from document.  We totally blocked
</blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote>on
<blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote>this issue and hope that can get support from you
so that can obtain a stable work from riak database for our
production.
Thank you so much.  Hope that can get your reply soon.

* The following is our riak node information :

Riak version:  2.2.0
OS :  CentOS Linux release 7.2.1511
Cpu :  4 core
Memory : 4G
Riak configure : the attached file &quot;riak.conf&quot;

</div><ol class="mailing-list-replies"><li class="mailing-list-reply"><details id="msg17957" class="mailing-list-message"><summary><header class="mailing-list-message__header"><span class="mailing-list-message__author">Alexander Sicular</span><time datetime="2017-02-10T05:16:09-08:00">February 10, 2017 at 5:16 AM</time><span class="mailing-list-message__subject">Re: [Basho Riak] Fail To Update Document Repeatly With Cluster of 5	Nodes</span></header></summary><div class="mailing-list-message__content"><div class="mailing-list-message__body">Yes. For a number of reasons a single Riak cluster is not designed to run
over a WAN (Riak EE is specifically designed to connect two or more
separate clusters over a WAN or LAN.)

On Fri, Feb 10, 2017 at 02:40 Nguyễn Văn Nghĩa Em &lt;nghi...@gmail.com&gt; wrote:

<blockquote>Hi All,

These nodes are not in Local Area Network because our host provider
doesn&#x27;t have a local IP. It is only our DEV environments. If there are
problems with cluster I will setup a local environment to try.

Do you think that it is the reason of our issues ?

Best Regards,

2017-02-10 3:21 GMT+07:00 DeadZen &lt;dead...@deadzen.com&gt;:

Why are they public?

On Thu, Feb 9, 2017 at 3:11 PM, Alexander Sicular &lt;sicul...@gmail.com&gt;
wrote:
<blockquote>Speaking of timings:

ring_members : [&#x27;riak-node1@64.137.190.244&#x27;,&#x27;riak-node2@64.137.247.82&#x27;,

Are these nodes in the same local area network?

On Thu, Feb 9, 2017 at 12:49 PM, my hue &lt;tranmyhue.grac...@gmail.com&gt;
</blockquote>wrote:
<blockquote><blockquote>Dear Russel,

I did the simplest possible with new document and use modify_type to
</blockquote></blockquote>update
<blockquote><blockquote>a single register.
I still meet fail update at some times.

My steps did as follow :

Step 1:   Initial a new document Map
Step 2:  Create new map with :  riakc_pb_socket:update_type(Pid,
{BucketType, Bucket}, Key,  riakc_map:to_op(Map), []).
Step 3:   Fetch to check result :
riakc_pb_socket:fetch_type(Pid,{BucketType,Bucket}, Key).
Step 4:  Create Fun for input of modify_type which update only one
</blockquote></blockquote>field of
<blockquote><blockquote>map

Fun1 = fun(OldMap) -&gt; riakc_map:update({&lt;&lt;&quot;status_id&quot;&gt;&gt;, register},
</blockquote></blockquote>fun(R)
<blockquote><blockquote>-&gt; riakc_register:set(&lt;&lt;&quot;show&quot;&gt;&gt;,  R) end, OldMap) end.

Fun2 = fun(OldMap) -&gt; riakc_map:update({&lt;&lt;&quot;status_id&quot;&gt;&gt;, register},
</blockquote></blockquote>fun(R)
<blockquote><blockquote>-&gt; riakc_register:set(&lt;&lt;&quot;hide&quot;&gt;&gt;,  R) end, OldMap) end.

Step 5: Update :

riakc_pb_socket:modify_type(Pid, Fun1, {BucketType, Bucket}, Key, []).

Fetch to check :

riakc_pb_socket:fetch_type(Pid,{BucketType,Bucket}, Key).

Step 6:  Update:

riakc_pb_socket:modify_type(Pid, Fun2, {BucketType, Bucket}, Key, []).

Fetch to check :

riakc_pb_socket:fetch_type(Pid,{BucketType,Bucket}, Key).

For my debug and test,  I repeated step 5 and step 6 on one document
</blockquote></blockquote>about
<blockquote><blockquote>20 times.
And via many documents, I meet weird behaviour that some documents meet
</blockquote></blockquote>fail
<blockquote><blockquote>update, and some documents never fail update.
The first time, I think that cause network, or timeout between nodes and
this is only random of fail.  So I deleted documents with command:

riakc_pb_socket:delete(Pid, {BucketType,Bucket}, Key, []).

Then retest on each document of first test again. And It is very amazing
that the documents meet fail at first test still meet fail at this
</blockquote></blockquote>second
<blockquote><blockquote>test, and the documents passed at first test still pass at this second
</blockquote></blockquote>test.
<blockquote><blockquote>Delete all again, and retest and of course get the same result.

After all I make other test case, I used one fail document at all test
times, and keep all fields except change key to get different documents
</blockquote></blockquote>for
<blockquote><blockquote>the debug.  And very surprise that I still got some fail and some
</blockquote></blockquote>success,
<blockquote><blockquote>although documents are  the same field and value except key.  Delete and
retest and still the same result. Documents succeeded will be always
succeed. And document meet fail will be always failed.  I totally do not
understand root cause till now. And hope that can get support and help
</blockquote></blockquote>from
<blockquote><blockquote>the developers of riak.   I can tell that my system mostly fail with
</blockquote></blockquote>cluster
<blockquote><blockquote>run when faced this issue.

</blockquote></blockquote>same
<blockquote><blockquote>bucket type and bucket.   Bucket Type and Bucket have properties with
</blockquote></blockquote>which
<blockquote><blockquote>I reported on first email. So for remind, under is a description  of
</blockquote></blockquote>bucket
<blockquote><blockquote>type, bucket and cluster :

* Bucket Type :

- Bucket type created with the following command:

riak-admin bucket-type create bucket_type_name
&#x27;{&quot;props&quot;:{&quot;backend&quot;:&quot;bitcask_mult&quot;,&quot;datatype&quot;:&quot;map&quot;}}&#x27;

riak-admin bucket-type activate bucket_type_name

* Bucket Property:

</blockquote></blockquote>{&quot;props&quot;:{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;bucket_name&quot;,&quot;active&quot;:true,&quot;allow_mult&quot;:true,&quot;backend&quot;:&quot;bitcask_mult&quot;,&quot;basic_quorum&quot;:false,&quot;big_vclock&quot;:50,&quot;chash_keyfun&quot;:{&quot;mod&quot;:&quot;riak_core_util&quot;,&quot;fun&quot;:&quot;chash_std_keyfun&quot;},&quot;claimant&quot;:&quot;
riak-node1@64.137.190.244
&quot;,&quot;datatype&quot;:&quot;map&quot;,&quot;dvv_enabled&quot;:true,&quot;dw&quot;:&quot;quorum&quot;,&quot;last_write_wins&quot;:false,&quot;linkfun&quot;:{&quot;mod&quot;:&quot;riak_kv_wm_link_walker&quot;,&quot;fun&quot;:&quot;mapreduce_linkfun&quot;},&quot;n_val&quot;:3,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;bucket_name&quot;,&quot;notfound_ok&quot;:true,&quot;old_vclock&quot;:86400,&quot;postcommit&quot;:[],&quot;pr&quot;:0,&quot;precommit&quot;:[],&quot;pw&quot;:0,&quot;r&quot;:&quot;quorum&quot;,&quot;rw&quot;:&quot;quorum&quot;,&quot;search_index&quot;:&quot;menu_idx&quot;,&quot;small_vclock&quot;:50,&quot;w&quot;:&quot;quorum&quot;,&quot;young_vclock&quot;:20}}
<blockquote><blockquote>
Note :
+ &quot;datatype&quot;:&quot;map&quot;
+ &quot;last_write_wins&quot;: false
+ &quot;dvv_enabled&quot;: true
+ &quot;allow_mult&quot;: true

* Cluster Infor :

- Member status :

<blockquote><blockquote>riak-admin member-status
</blockquote></blockquote>

==================================

On Tue, Feb 7, 2017 at 2:11 AM, John Daily &lt;jda...@basho.com&gt;
wrote:
Originally I suspected the context which allows Riak to resolve
conflicts was not present in your data, but I see it in your
</blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote>map structure.
<blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote>Thanks for supplying such a detailed description.

How fast is your turnaround time between an update and a fetch?
Even if the cluster is healthy it’s not impossible to see a
</blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote>timeout between
<blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote>nodes, which could result in a stale retrieval. Have you
</blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote>verified whether
<blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote>the stale data persists?

A single node cluster gives an advantage that you’ll never see
</blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote>in
<blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote>a real cluster: a perfectly synchronized clock. It also
</blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote>reduces (but does
<blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote>not completely eliminate) the possibility of an internal
</blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote>timeout between
<blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote>processes.

-John

<blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote>wrote:

Dear Riak Team,

I and my team used riak as database for my production with an
cluster including 5 nodes.
While production run, we meet an critical bug that is
</blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote>sometimes
<blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote>fail to update document.
I and my colleagues performed debug and detected an issue with
the scenario as follow:

+  fetch document
+  change value of document
+  update document

Repeat about 10 times and will meet fail. With the document is
updated continually,
sometimes will face update fail.

The first time,  5 nodes of cluster we used riak version
</blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote>2.1.1.
<blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote>After meet above bug, we upgraded to use riak version 2.2.0
</blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote>and
<blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote>this issue still occurs.

Via many time test,  debug using  Tcpdump at riak node :

tcpdump -A -ttt  -i {interface} src host {host} and dst port
{port}

And together with the command:

riak-admin status | grep &quot;node_puts_map\|
</blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote>node_puts_map_total\|
<blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote>node_puts_total\| vnode_map_update_total\| vnode_puts_total\&quot;

we  got that the riak server already get the update request.
However, do not know why riak backend fail to update document.
At the fail time,  from riak server log everything is ok.

Then we removed cluster and use a single riak server,  and see
that above bug never happen.

For that reason, think that is only happen with cluster work.
</blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote>We
<blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote>took research on basho riak document and our riak configure
seems that like suggestion from document.  We totally blocked
</blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote>on
<blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote>this issue and hope that can get support from you
so that can obtain a stable work from riak database for our
production.
Thank you so much.  Hope that can get your reply soon.

* The following is our riak node information :

Riak version:  2.2.0
OS :  CentOS Linux release 7.2.1511
Cpu :  4 core
Memory : 4G
Riak configure : the attached file &quot;riak.conf&quot;

Note :

- We mostly using default configure of riak configure except
</blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote>that
<blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote>we used storage backend is multi

multi_backend.bitcask_mult.storage_backend = bitcask
multi_backend.bitcask_mult.bitcask.data_root =
/var/lib/riak/bitcask_mult

earlier message in case there is a genuine bug lurking somewhere, but yes,
running inside a single network will make Riak much happier.

-John

<blockquote>On Feb 9, 2017, at 9:01 PM, Nguyễn Văn Nghĩa Em &lt;nghi...@gmail.com&gt; wrote:

These nodes are not in Local Area Network because our host provider doesn&#x27;t
have a local IP. It is only our DEV environments. If there are problems with
cluster I will setup a local environment to try.

Best Regards,

Are these nodes in the same local area network?

On Thu, Feb 9, 2017 at 12:49 PM, my hue &lt;tranmyhue.grac...@gmail.com
&lt;mailto:tranmyhue.grac...@gmail.com&gt;&gt; wrote:
<blockquote>Dear Russel,

I did the simplest possible with new document and use modify_type to update
a single register.
I still meet fail update at some times.

My steps did as follow :

Step 1:   Initial a new document Map
Step 2:  Create new map with :  riakc_pb_socket:update_type(Pid,
{BucketType, Bucket}, Key,  riakc_map:to_op(Map), []).
Step 3:   Fetch to check result :
riakc_pb_socket:fetch_type(Pid,{BucketType,Bucket}, Key).
Step 4:  Create Fun for input of modify_type which update only one field of
map

Fun1 = fun(OldMap) -&gt; riakc_map:update({&lt;&lt;&quot;status_id&quot;&gt;&gt;, register}, fun(R)
-&gt; riakc_register:set(&lt;&lt;&quot;show&quot;&gt;&gt;,  R) end, OldMap) end.

Fun2 = fun(OldMap) -&gt; riakc_map:update({&lt;&lt;&quot;status_id&quot;&gt;&gt;, register}, fun(R)
-&gt; riakc_register:set(&lt;&lt;&quot;hide&quot;&gt;&gt;,  R) end, OldMap) end.

Step 5: Update :

riakc_pb_socket:modify_type(Pid, Fun1, {BucketType, Bucket}, Key, []).

Fetch to check :

riakc_pb_socket:fetch_type(Pid,{BucketType,Bucket}, Key).

Step 6:  Update:

riakc_pb_socket:modify_type(Pid, Fun2, {BucketType, Bucket}, Key, []).

Fetch to check :

riakc_pb_socket:fetch_type(Pid,{BucketType,Bucket}, Key).

For my debug and test,  I repeated step 5 and step 6 on one document about
20 times.
And via many documents, I meet weird behaviour that some documents meet
fail
update, and some documents never fail update.
The first time, I think that cause network, or timeout between nodes and
this is only random of fail.  So I deleted documents with command:

riakc_pb_socket:delete(Pid, {BucketType,Bucket}, Key, []).

Then retest on each document of first test again. And It is very amazing
that the documents meet fail at first test still meet fail at this second
test, and the documents passed at first test still pass at this second
test.
Delete all again, and retest and of course get the same result.

After all I make other test case, I used one fail document at all test
times, and keep all fields except change key to get different documents for
the debug.  And very surprise that I still got some fail and some success,
although documents are  the same field and value except key.  Delete and
retest and still the same result. Documents succeeded will be always
succeed. And document meet fail will be always failed.  I totally do not
understand root cause till now. And hope that can get support and help from
the developers of riak.   I can tell that my system mostly fail with
cluster
run when faced this issue.

The following is some map documents I used on the test.  And I also
attached
the extracted log of each node at one of the fail times together with this
email. I do not really get meaning of riak log but hope that can help
developers of riak get something.

On Tue, Feb 7, 2017 at 2:11 AM, John Daily &lt;jda...@basho.com
&lt;mailto:jda...@basho.com&gt;&gt;
wrote:
Originally I suspected the context which allows Riak to resolve
conflicts was not present in your data, but I see it in your map
structure.
Thanks for supplying such a detailed description.

How fast is your turnaround time between an update and a fetch?
Even if the cluster is healthy it’s not impossible to see a
timeout between
nodes, which could result in a stale retrieval. Have you verified
whether
the stale data persists?

A single node cluster gives an advantage that you’ll never see in
a real cluster: a perfectly synchronized clock. It also reduces
(but does
not completely eliminate) the possibility of an internal timeout
between
processes.

-John

<blockquote>On Feb 6, 2017, at 1:02 PM, my hue &lt;tranmyhue.grac...@gmail.com
&lt;mailto:tranmyhue.grac...@gmail.com&gt;&gt;
wrote:

Dear Riak Team,

I and my team used riak as database for my production with an
cluster including 5 nodes.
While production run, we meet an critical bug that is sometimes
fail to update document.
I and my colleagues performed debug and detected an issue with
the scenario as follow:

+  fetch document
+  change value of document
+  update document

Repeat about 10 times and will meet fail. With the document is
updated continually,
sometimes will face update fail.

The first time,  5 nodes of cluster we used riak version 2.1.1.
After meet above bug, we upgraded to use riak version 2.2.0 and
this issue still occurs.

Via many time test,  debug using  Tcpdump at riak node :

tcpdump -A -ttt  -i {interface} src host {host} and dst port
{port}

And together with the command:

riak-admin status | grep &quot;node_puts_map\| node_puts_map_total\|
node_puts_total\| vnode_map_update_total\| vnode_puts_total\&quot;

we  got that the riak server already get the update request.
However, do not know why riak backend fail to update document.
At the fail time,  from riak server log everything is ok.

Then we removed cluster and use a single riak server,  and see
that above bug never happen.

For that reason, think that is only happen with cluster work. We
took research on basho riak document and our riak configure
seems that like suggestion from document.  We totally blocked on
this issue and hope that can get support from you
so that can obtain a stable work from riak database for our
production.
Thank you so much.  Hope that can get your reply soon.

</blockquote></blockquote>update
<blockquote><blockquote>a single register.

My steps did as follow :

Step 1:   Initial a new document Map
Step 2:  Create new map with :  riakc_pb_socket:update_type(Pid,
{BucketType, Bucket}, Key,  riakc_map:to_op(Map), []).
Step 3:   Fetch to check result :
riakc_pb_socket:fetch_type(Pid,{BucketType,Bucket}, Key).
Step 4:  Create Fun for input of modify_type which update only one
</blockquote></blockquote>field of
<blockquote><blockquote>map

Fun1 = fun(OldMap) -&gt; riakc_map:update({&lt;&lt;&quot;status_id&quot;&gt;&gt;, register},
</blockquote></blockquote>fun(R)
<blockquote><blockquote>-&gt; riakc_register:set(&lt;&lt;&quot;show&quot;&gt;&gt;,  R) end, OldMap) end.

Fun2 = fun(OldMap) -&gt; riakc_map:update({&lt;&lt;&quot;status_id&quot;&gt;&gt;, register},
</blockquote></blockquote>fun(R)
<blockquote><blockquote>-&gt; riakc_register:set(&lt;&lt;&quot;hide&quot;&gt;&gt;,  R) end, OldMap) end.

Step 5: Update :

riakc_pb_socket:modify_type(Pid, Fun1, {BucketType, Bucket}, Key, []).

Fetch to check :

riakc_pb_socket:fetch_type(Pid,{BucketType,Bucket}, Key).

Step 6:  Update:

riakc_pb_socket:modify_type(Pid, Fun2, {BucketType, Bucket}, Key, []).

Fetch to check :

riakc_pb_socket:fetch_type(Pid,{BucketType,Bucket}, Key).

For my debug and test,  I repeated step 5 and step 6 on one document
</blockquote></blockquote>about

wrote:
Originally I suspected the context which allows Riak to
</blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote>resolve
<blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote>conflicts was not present in your data, but I see it in your
</blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote>map structure.
<blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote>Thanks for supplying such a detailed description.

How fast is your turnaround time between an update and a
</blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote>fetch?
<blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote>Even if the cluster is healthy it’s not impossible to see a
</blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote>timeout between
<blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote>nodes, which could result in a stale retrieval. Have you
</blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote>verified whether
<blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote>the stale data persists?

A single node cluster gives an advantage that you’ll never
</blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote>see in
<blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote>a real cluster: a perfectly synchronized clock. It also
</blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote>reduces (but does
<blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote>not completely eliminate) the possibility of an internal
</blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote>timeout between
<blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote>processes.

-John

<blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote>wrote:

Dear Riak Team,

I and my team used riak as database for my production with an
cluster including 5 nodes.
While production run, we meet an critical bug that is
</blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote>sometimes
<blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote>fail to update document.
I and my colleagues performed debug and detected an issue
</blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote>with
<blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote>the scenario as follow:

+  fetch document
+  change value of document
+  update document

Repeat about 10 times and will meet fail. With the document
</blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote>is
<blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote>updated continually,
sometimes will face update fail.

The first time,  5 nodes of cluster we used riak version
</blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote>2.1.1.
<blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote>After meet above bug, we upgraded to use riak version 2.2.0
</blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote>and
<blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote>this issue still occurs.

Via many time test,  debug using  Tcpdump at riak node :

tcpdump -A -ttt  -i {interface} src host {host} and dst port
{port}

And together with the command:

riak-admin status | grep &quot;node_puts_map\|
</blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote>node_puts_map_total\|
<blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote>node_puts_total\| vnode_map_update_total\| vnode_puts_total\&quot;

we  got that the riak server already get the update request.
However, do not know why riak backend fail to update
</blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote>document.
<blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote>At the fail time,  from riak server log everything is ok.

Then we removed cluster and use a single riak server,  and
</blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote>see
<blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote>that above bug never happen.

For that reason, think that is only happen with cluster
</blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote>work. We
<blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote>took research on basho riak document and our riak configure
seems that like suggestion from document.  We totally
</blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote>blocked on
<blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote>this issue and hope that can get support from you
so that can obtain a stable work from riak database for our
production.
Thank you so much.  Hope that can get your reply soon.

* The following is our riak node information :

Riak configure : the attached file &quot;riak.conf&quot;

<blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote>
* The riak client :

+ riak-erlang-client:
<a href="https://github.com/basho/riak-erlang-client">https://github.com/basho/riak-erlang-client</a>
+ release :   2.4.2

------------------------------------------------------------
</blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote>-----------------------------------------------------------------
<blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote>
* Riak client API used:

+ Insert/Update:

riakc_pb_socket:update_type(Pid, {Bucket-Type, Bucket}, Key,
riakc_map:to_op(Map), []).

+ Fetch :

riakc_pb_socket:fetch_type(Pid, {BucketType, Bucket}, Key,
</blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote>[]).
<blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote>
------------------------------------------------------------
</blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote>-----------------------------------------------------------------
<blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote>
* Step to perform an  update :

- Fetch document
- Update document

------------------------------------------------------------

riak-users@lists.basho.com

</div><ol class="mailing-list-replies"><li class="mailing-list-reply"><details id="msg17961" class="mailing-list-message"><summary><header class="mailing-list-message__header"><span class="mailing-list-message__author">John Daily</span><time datetime="2017-02-11T14:17:08-08:00">February 11, 2017 at 2:17 PM</time><span class="mailing-list-message__subject">Re: [Basho Riak] Fail To Update Document Repeatly With Cluster of 5	Nodes</span></header></summary><div class="mailing-list-message__content"><div class="mailing-list-message__body">It definitely sounds as if your nodes are having problems talking to each
other, as suspected. Putting them on a single network is a significant
improvement.

The default values for pw and pr are 0; Riak by default prefers to always be
available, even if the cluster is in a degraded state. This can, as you have
demonstrated, mean that you see stale data because the portion of the cluster
that you are writing data to or reading data from is not able to communicate
with the rest of the cluster.

You must decide what tolerance you have for stale data to decide whether to
increase pr and pw.

I strongly recommend reading these documents:

<a href="http://docs.basho.com/riak/kv/2.2.0/learn/concepts/eventual-consistency/">http://docs.basho.com/riak/kv/2.2.0/learn/concepts/eventual-consistency/</a>
&lt;<a href="http://docs.basho.com/riak/kv/2.2.0/learn/concepts/eventual-consistency/">http://docs.basho.com/riak/kv/2.2.0/learn/concepts/eventual-consistency/</a>&gt;
<a href="http://basho.com/tag/configurable-behaviors/">http://basho.com/tag/configurable-behaviors/</a>
&lt;<a href="http://basho.com/tag/configurable-behaviors/">http://basho.com/tag/configurable-behaviors/</a>&gt;

The blog posts are quite old by now, but the ideas are still useful.

-John

<blockquote>Dear Russel,

I did the simplest possible with new document and use modify_type to
update
a single register.
I still meet fail update at some times.

My steps did as follow :

Step 1:   Initial a new document Map
Step 2:  Create new map with :  riakc_pb_socket:update_type(Pid,
{BucketType, Bucket}, Key,  riakc_map:to_op(Map), []).
Step 3:   Fetch to check result :
riakc_pb_socket:fetch_type(Pid,{BucketType,Bucket}, Key).
Step 4:  Create Fun for input of modify_type which update only one field
of
map

Fun1 = fun(OldMap) -&gt; riakc_map:update({&lt;&lt;&quot;status_id&quot;&gt;&gt;, register}, fun(R)
-&gt; riakc_register:set(&lt;&lt;&quot;show&quot;&gt;&gt;,  R) end, OldMap) end.

Fun2 = fun(OldMap) -&gt; riakc_map:update({&lt;&lt;&quot;status_id&quot;&gt;&gt;, register}, fun(R)
-&gt; riakc_register:set(&lt;&lt;&quot;hide&quot;&gt;&gt;,  R) end, OldMap) end.

Step 5: Update :

riakc_pb_socket:modify_type(Pid, Fun1, {BucketType, Bucket}, Key, []).

Fetch to check :

riakc_pb_socket:fetch_type(Pid,{BucketType,Bucket}, Key).

Step 6:  Update:

riakc_pb_socket:modify_type(Pid, Fun2, {BucketType, Bucket}, Key, []).

Fetch to check :

riakc_pb_socket:fetch_type(Pid,{BucketType,Bucket}, Key).

For my debug and test,  I repeated step 5 and step 6 on one document about
20 times.
And via many documents, I meet weird behaviour that some documents meet
fail
update, and some documents never fail update.
The first time, I think that cause network, or timeout between nodes and
this is only random of fail.  So I deleted documents with command:

riakc_pb_socket:delete(Pid, {BucketType,Bucket}, Key, []).

Then retest on each document of first test again. And It is very amazing
that the documents meet fail at first test still meet fail at this second
test, and the documents passed at first test still pass at this second
test.
Delete all again, and retest and of course get the same result.

After all I make other test case, I used one fail document at all test
times, and keep all fields except change key to get different documents
for
the debug.  And very surprise that I still got some fail and some success,
although documents are  the same field and value except key.  Delete and
retest and still the same result. Documents succeeded will be always
succeed. And document meet fail will be always failed.  I totally do not
understand root cause till now. And hope that can get support and help
from
the developers of riak.   I can tell that my system mostly fail with
cluster
run when faced this issue.

The following is some map documents I used on the test.  And I also
attached
the extracted log of each node at one of the fail times together with this
email. I do not really get meaning of riak log but hope that can help
developers of riak get something.

{{&lt;&lt;&quot;created_time_dt&quot;&gt;&gt;,register},

On Tue, Feb 7, 2017 at 2:11 AM, John Daily &lt;jda...@basho.com
&lt;mailto:jda...@basho.com&gt;&gt;
wrote:
Originally I suspected the context which allows Riak to resolve
conflicts was not present in your data, but I see it in your map
structure.
Thanks for supplying such a detailed description.

How fast is your turnaround time between an update and a fetch?
Even if the cluster is healthy it’s not impossible to see a
timeout between
nodes, which could result in a stale retrieval. Have you
verified whether
the stale data persists?

A single node cluster gives an advantage that you’ll never see in
a real cluster: a perfectly synchronized clock. It also reduces
(but does
not completely eliminate) the possibility of an internal timeout
between
processes.

-John

<blockquote>On Feb 6, 2017, at 1:02 PM, my hue &lt;tranmyhue.grac...@gmail.com
&lt;mailto:tranmyhue.grac...@gmail.com&gt;&gt;
wrote:

Dear Riak Team,

I and my team used riak as database for my production with an
cluster including 5 nodes.
While production run, we meet an critical bug that is sometimes
fail to update document.
I and my colleagues performed debug and detected an issue with
the scenario as follow:

+  fetch document
+  change value of document
+  update document

Repeat about 10 times and will meet fail. With the document is
updated continually,
sometimes will face update fail.

The first time,  5 nodes of cluster we used riak version 2.1.1.
After meet above bug, we upgraded to use riak version 2.2.0 and
this issue still occurs.

Via many time test,  debug using  Tcpdump at riak node :

tcpdump -A -ttt  -i {interface} src host {host} and dst port
{port}

And together with the command:

riak-admin status | grep &quot;node_puts_map\| node_puts_map_total\|
node_puts_total\| vnode_map_update_total\| vnode_puts_total\&quot;

we  got that the riak server already get the update request.
However, do not know why riak backend fail to update document.
At the fail time,  from riak server log everything is ok.

Then we removed cluster and use a single riak server,  and see
that above bug never happen.

For that reason, think that is only happen with cluster work. We
took research on basho riak document and our riak configure
seems that like suggestion from document.  We totally blocked on
this issue and hope that can get support from you
so that can obtain a stable work from riak database for our
production.
Thank you so much.  Hope that can get your reply soon.

<blockquote><blockquote>Dear Russel,

I did the simplest possible with new document and use modify_type to
</blockquote></blockquote>update
<blockquote><blockquote>a single register.
I still meet fail update at some times.

My steps did as follow :

Step 1:   Initial a new document Map
Step 2:  Create new map with :  riakc_pb_socket:update_type(Pid,
{BucketType, Bucket}, Key,  riakc_map:to_op(Map), []).
Step 3:   Fetch to check result :
riakc_pb_socket:fetch_type(Pid,{BucketType,Bucket}, Key).
Step 4:  Create Fun for input of modify_type which update only one
</blockquote></blockquote>field of
<blockquote><blockquote>map

Fun1 = fun(OldMap) -&gt; riakc_map:update({&lt;&lt;&quot;status_id&quot;&gt;&gt;, register},
</blockquote></blockquote>fun(R)
<blockquote><blockquote>-&gt; riakc_register:set(&lt;&lt;&quot;show&quot;&gt;&gt;,  R) end, OldMap) end.

Fun2 = fun(OldMap) -&gt; riakc_map:update({&lt;&lt;&quot;status_id&quot;&gt;&gt;, register},
</blockquote></blockquote>fun(R)
<blockquote><blockquote>-&gt; riakc_register:set(&lt;&lt;&quot;hide&quot;&gt;&gt;,  R) end, OldMap) end.

Step 5: Update :

riakc_pb_socket:modify_type(Pid, Fun1, {BucketType, Bucket}, Key, []).

Fetch to check :

riakc_pb_socket:fetch_type(Pid,{BucketType,Bucket}, Key).

Step 6:  Update:

riakc_pb_socket:modify_type(Pid, Fun2, {BucketType, Bucket}, Key, []).

Fetch to check :

riakc_pb_socket:fetch_type(Pid,{BucketType,Bucket}, Key).

For my debug and test,  I repeated step 5 and step 6 on one document
</blockquote></blockquote>about
<blockquote><blockquote>20 times.
And via many documents, I meet weird behaviour that some documents
</blockquote></blockquote>meet fail
<blockquote><blockquote>update, and some documents never fail update.
The first time, I think that cause network, or timeout between nodes
</blockquote></blockquote>and
<blockquote><blockquote>this is only random of fail.  So I deleted documents with command:

riakc_pb_socket:delete(Pid, {BucketType,Bucket}, Key, []).

Then retest on each document of first test again. And It is very
</blockquote></blockquote>amazing
<blockquote><blockquote>that the documents meet fail at first test still meet fail at this
</blockquote></blockquote>second
<blockquote><blockquote>test, and the documents passed at first test still pass at this second
</blockquote></blockquote>test.
<blockquote><blockquote>Delete all again, and retest and of course get the same result.

After all I make other test case, I used one fail document at all test
times, and keep all fields except change key to get different
</blockquote></blockquote>documents for
<blockquote><blockquote>the debug.  And very surprise that I still got some fail and some
</blockquote></blockquote>success,
<blockquote><blockquote>although documents are  the same field and value except key.  Delete
</blockquote></blockquote>and
<blockquote><blockquote>retest and still the same result. Documents succeeded will be always
succeed. And document meet fail will be always failed.  I totally do
</blockquote></blockquote>not
<blockquote><blockquote>understand root cause till now. And hope that can get support and help
</blockquote></blockquote>from
<blockquote><blockquote>the developers of riak.   I can tell that my system mostly fail with
</blockquote></blockquote>cluster
<blockquote><blockquote>run when faced this issue.

Originally I suspected the context which allows Riak to
</blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote>resolve
<blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote>conflicts was not present in your data, but I see it in your
</blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote>map structure.
<blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote>Thanks for supplying such a detailed description.

How fast is your turnaround time between an update and a
</blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote>fetch?
<blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote>Even if the cluster is healthy it’s not impossible to see a
</blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote>timeout between
<blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote>nodes, which could result in a stale retrieval. Have you
</blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote>verified whether
<blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote>the stale data persists?

A single node cluster gives an advantage that you’ll never
</blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote>see in
<blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote>a real cluster: a perfectly synchronized clock. It also
</blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote>reduces (but does
<blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote>not completely eliminate) the possibility of an internal
</blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote>timeout between
<blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote>processes.

-John

<blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote>wrote:

Dear Riak Team,

I and my team used riak as database for my production with an
cluster including 5 nodes.
While production run, we meet an critical bug that is
</blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote>sometimes
<blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote>fail to update document.
I and my colleagues performed debug and detected an issue
</blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote>with
<blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote>the scenario as follow:

+  fetch document
+  change value of document
+  update document

Repeat about 10 times and will meet fail. With the document
</blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote>is
<blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote>updated continually,
sometimes will face update fail.

The first time,  5 nodes of cluster we used riak version
</blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote>2.1.1.
<blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote>After meet above bug, we upgraded to use riak version 2.2.0
</blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote>and
<blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote>this issue still occurs.

Via many time test,  debug using  Tcpdump at riak node :

tcpdump -A -ttt  -i {interface} src host {host} and dst port
{port}

And together with the command:

riak-admin status | grep &quot;node_puts_map\|
</blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote>node_puts_map_total\|
<blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote>node_puts_total\| vnode_map_update_total\| vnode_puts_total\&quot;

we  got that the riak server already get the update request.
However, do not know why riak backend fail to update
</blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote>document.
<blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote>At the fail time,  from riak server log everything is ok.

Then we removed cluster and use a single riak server,  and
</blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote>see
<blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote>that above bug never happen.

For that reason, think that is only happen with cluster
</blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote>work. We
<blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote>took research on basho riak document and our riak configure
seems that like suggestion from document.  We totally
</blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote>blocked on
<blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote>this issue and hope that can get support from you
so that can obtain a stable work from riak database for our
production.
Thank you so much.  Hope that can get your reply soon.

* The following is our riak node information :

Riak version:  2.2.0
OS :  CentOS Linux release 7.2.1511
Cpu :  4 core
Memory : 4G
Riak configure : the attached file &quot;riak.conf&quot;

Note :

- We mostly using default configure of riak configure except
</blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote></blockquote>that
<blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote><blockquote>we used storage backend is multi
